\displaystyle=\mathsf{vect}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}

\displaystyle=\Rep_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)).

(by Example
A.24
)

In particular, the Tannaka dual of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(\Spec(k))^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}

is again
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
.
∎

This example justifies the following notation:

Notation 3.12
.

Let
x\in X(\bar{k})
be a closed point.

•

We let
\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)
denote the Tannaka dual of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
with respect to the fibre functor at
x
.

•

We let
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X,x)
denote the Tannaka dual of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
with respect to the fibre functor at
x
, and we call it
Ayoub’s motivic Galois group
of
X
with base-point
x
.

Under Tannaka duality, the forgetful functor (
25
) corresponds to a homomorphism of pro-algebraic groups

\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)\rightarrow\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X,x).

(26)

Remark 3.13
.

As a consequence of Proposition
4.5
below, the shifted inverse image functor

a_{X}^{*}[d]\colon\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(\Spec(k))^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}

admits a right adjoint, defined once again by the formula

{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ a_{X,*}[-d]\colon\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(\Spec(k))^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}.

Using the same argument as in Remark
1.30
, we deduce that
a_{X}^{*}[d]
is fully faithful, with essential image stable under subquotients.
This yields a canonical surjection between Tannaka dual groups

\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X,x)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k),

(27)

where the right-hand side was computed in Example
3.11
.

By Theorem
3.6
, the motivic Galois action on constructible complexes is faithful;
in view of Remark
3.8
, this happens already on local systems.
One can thus expect that it is not straightforward to describe the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-orbit of a general object
L\in\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
.
The simplest non-trivial case is the following:

Example 3.14
.

As in Example
2.20
, fix a finite Galois extension
k^{\prime}/k
, set
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{k}k^{\prime}
, and write
e_{X}\colon X^{\prime}\to X
for the corresponding finite étale covering.
We regard
X^{\prime}
as a
k
-variety, and we want to analyse the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on the unit local system

{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma,X^{\prime}}:=\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma,X^{\prime}}[d]\in\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime}).

Since the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on sheaves of geometric origin is monoidal, it necessarily preserves
{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma,X^{\prime}}
:
more precisely, the unit local system canonically underlies an object of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
(see Corollary
4.7
below).
Under the monoidal equivalence

\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})=\prod_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Hom_{\sigma}(k^{\prime},\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})

induced by Lemma
1.22
, the object
{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma,X^{\prime}}
corresponds to the unit tuple
\oplus_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Hom_{\sigma}(k^{\prime},\mathbb{C})}{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma^{\prime},X^{\prime}}
.

Even though the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action preserves
{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma,X^{\prime}}
, it does not preserve its direct summands individually, as we now explain.
For every
\sigma^{\prime}\in\Hom_{\sigma}(k^{\prime},\mathbb{C})
, the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
-action induced by the homomorphism (
23
) necessarily preserves the corresponding summand
{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma^{\prime},X^{\prime}}
.
The image of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
coincides with the kernel of the projection
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)\twoheadrightarrow\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
(by Proposition
3.5
) and,
in particular, is independent of the choice of
\sigma^{\prime}
.
It follows that the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma,X^{\prime}}
factors through a
\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
-action permuting its direct summands.

We want to describe this permutation action explicitly.
Recall from Example
2.10
that the identification of
\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
with a quotient of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
depends on the choice of an element
\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\in\Hom_{\sigma}(k^{\prime},\mathbb{C})
.
From now on, fix such a choice.
We rename the various unit summands by setting

{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma^{\prime},X^{\prime}}:=s{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0},X^{\prime}}

where
s\in\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
is the unique element satisfying
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\circ s
.
We claim that, with this notation, the
\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
-action on
{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma,X^{\prime}}
satisfies the formula

r\cdot(s{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0},X^{\prime}})=(rs){{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0},X^{\prime}}

for all
r,s\in\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
.
Using the compatibility of the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action with inverse images, it suffices to establish the claim when
X=\Spec(k)
.
In this case, using the compatibility of the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action with direct images, the claim reduces
to the computations of Example
2.10
(see also Remark
2.11
).
Here, we are implicitly invoking Theorem
5.8
(and Lemma
5.9
) in order to use the input from Nori’s theory.
∎

3.3
Proof of the fundamental sequence

As before, let
X
be a smooth, geometrically connected
k
-variety of dimension
d
and structural morphism
a_{X}\colon X\to\Spec(k)
.
The goal now is to establish the analogue of Theorem
2.24
for Ayoub’s motivic Galois group
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X,x)
.
Before stating the theorem, we discuss some preliminary results, along the lines of Section
2.3
.

The easiest way to produce an object of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
is by considering the Betti realization of a dualizable motivic sheaf
(this is justified by Corollary
4.7
below).

Example 3.15
.

Let us consider again the setting of Example
2.20
:
fix a finite Galois extension
k^{\prime}/k
, set
X^{\prime}\times_{k}k^{\prime}
, and write
e_{X}\colon X^{\prime}\to X
for the corresponding finite étale covering.
The local system

{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{X^{\prime}/X}:=\Bti_{X}^{*}(e_{X,*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{X^{\prime}}[d])\in\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)

canonically underlies an equivariant object
{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{X^{\prime}/X}\in\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
.
As in Example
2.20
, the Tannakian subcategory
\langle{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{X^{\prime}/X}\rangle^{\otimes}\subset\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
is equivalent to
\Rep_{\mathbb{Q}}(\textup{Gal}(X^{\prime}/X))
, with the generator
{{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{X^{\prime}/X}
corresponding to the regular representation.
This determines a surjection between Tannaka dual groups

\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X,x)\twoheadrightarrow\textup{Gal}(X^{\prime}/X).

(28)

To see this, note that the
\textup{Gal}(X^{\prime}/X)
-action on the local system
\omega_{X}({{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{X^{\prime}/X})
can be expressed in terms of the six operations, as in Example
2.8
.
Since the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action of Theorem
3.6
commutes with the six operations, the
\textup{Gal}(X^{\prime}/X)
-action lifts to the equivariant level.
Hence, we obtain a canonical
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra homomorphism

\mathbb{Q}[\textup{Gal}(X^{\prime}/X)]\to\End_{\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}}({{}^{p}\mathbb{Q}}_{X^{\prime}/X}),

and, as in Example
2.20
, the key point is to show that this is an isomorphism.
This can be achieved by repeating the argument of Example
2.20
in the setting of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-equivariant local systems.
Here again, we are implicitly invoking Theorem
5.8
(and Lemma
5.9
) to use the input from Nori’s theory.
∎

Now choose a complex embedding
\sigma^{\prime}\colon k^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
extending
\sigma
, and let
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})
denote the associated motivic Galois group.
Recall that, by Proposition
3.5
, we have a short exact sequence

1\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)\to\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)\to 1.

Note that the canonical equivalence
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})
of Lemma
1.33
(1) is
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})
-equivariant:
this is because, under the identifications of Corollary
1.19
, it comes from the functor

\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X;\mathcal{B}_{\sigma,X})\to\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X^{\prime};\mathcal{B}_{\sigma^{\prime},X^{\prime}})

induced by the isomorphism of Proposition
1.5
.
This leads to the following analogue of Lemma
2.22
:

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
k^{\prime}/k
be a finite Galois extension.
Given a smooth
k
-variety
X
, set
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{k}k^{\prime}
, and write
e_{X}\colon X^{\prime}\to X
for the corresponding finite Galois covering.
Then:

1.

The Tannakian categories
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})}
and
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
(defined by regarding
X^{\prime}
as a
k^{\prime}
-variety or as a
k
-variety, respectively) are canonically equivalent.

2.

The inverse image functor
e_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
induces a canonical equivalence

\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)})^{\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)},

where the
\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
is induced from its action on
X^{\prime}
via
k
-automorphisms.

Proof.

As usual, the second part follows from the fact that the fibered category
X\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
is a stack for the étale topology:
same argument as for Lemma
1.33
(2).

Let us focus on the first statement.
For the duration of the proof, we rename the chosen complex embedding
\sigma^{\prime}\colon k^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
as
\sigma^{\prime}_{0}
and let the symbol
\sigma^{\prime}
denote a generic element of
\Hom_{\sigma}(k^{\prime},\mathbb{C})
.
Consider the direct product decomposition

\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})=\prod_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\Hom_{\sigma}(k^{\prime},\mathbb{C})}\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})

induced by Lemma
1.22
.
By construction, the projection
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})
is
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})
-equivariant.
Passing to the equivariant objects, we get a canonical monoidal functor

\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})},

where the first passage is restriction under the homomorphism
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
.
We want to show that the composite functor is an equivalence.
By faithfully flat descent, it suffices to show this after extending coefficients from
\mathbb{Q}
to
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
.
We want to apply Proposition
A.27
to the short exact sequence of Proposition
3.5
.
To this end, we claim that the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})
is induced from the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})
in the sense of Example
A.17
.
The advantage of working with
\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
-coefficients is that it ensures the existence of a scheme-theoretic section to the projection
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)\twoheadrightarrow\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
, which is needed to make the discussion of Example
A.17
go through (see Remark
A.28
).

Let us prove the claim.
Following Example
3.14
, we rename the various factors by setting

\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})=:s\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime}),

where
s\in\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
is the unique element satisfying
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\circ s
.
By Remark
A.18
, proving the claim reduces to checking that, given an element
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
mapping to
r\in\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
, the functor
g\cdot-\colon\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})
sends
s\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})\mapsto(rs)\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})
for all
s\in\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
.
Recall that the subcategory
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})\subset\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})
can be characterized as the image of the endofunctor
\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime},X^{\prime}}\otimes-
(see Remark
1.8
).
Since the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action is compatible with the tensor product, proving the claim reduces to showing that, given an element
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)(\bar{\mathbb{Q}})
mapping to
r\in\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
, the formula

g\cdot s\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0},X^{\prime}}=(rs)\mathbb{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime}_{0},X^{\prime}}

holds for each
s\in\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
.
But this has been already proved in Example
3.14
.
∎

Using Lemma
3.16
(1), we obtain a monoidal functor

\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\xrightarrow{e_{X}^{*}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}=\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})}

compatible with the fibre functors at any closed point
x\in X(\bar{k})
.
This induces a canonical homomorphism between Tannaka dual groups

\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X^{\prime},x^{\prime})\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X,x),

(29)

where
x^{\prime}\in(X^{\prime})^{\sigma^{\prime}}
denotes the point corresponding to
x\in X^{\sigma}
.
We have the following generalization of Proposition
3.5
:

Proposition 3.17
.

Keep the notation and assumptions of Example
3.15
.
The sequence of pro-algebraic groups

1\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X^{\prime},x^{\prime})\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X,x)\to\textup{Gal}(X^{\prime}/X)\to 1

defined by the homomorphisms (
28
) and (
29
) is exact.

Proof.

Same argument as for Proposition
2.23
, using Example
3.15
in place of Example
2.20
.
∎

We can finally state the main result of this section:

Theorem 3.18
.

Let
X
be a smooth, geometrically connected
k
-variety.
Then, for every point
x\in X(\bar{k})
, the sequence of pro-algebraic groups

1\to\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(X,x)\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)\to 1

(30)

defined by the homomorphisms (
26
) and (
27
) is exact.

Proof.

It suffices to treat the case when the base-point
x
is a
k
-rational point:
once the thesis is proved in this case, the general case follows from the same argument used in the proof of Theorem
2.24
, with Proposition
3.17
playing here the same role as Proposition
2.23
there.

Let us focus on the case when
x
is
k
-rational.
Since the sequence in the statement is obtained via Construction
A.37
, it suffices to check that the hypotheses of Proposition
A.38
are satisfied:
we have to show that the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
is induced by an algebraic action of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
on
\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)
(in the sense of Example
A.33
).
By Lemma
A.43
, this is the case if and only if the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
admits a concretization
\gamma
with respect to the fibre functor at
x
(in the sense of Definition
A.41
).

To conclude the proof, we exhibit such a concretization.
For every
\Lambda\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}
and every
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)(\Lambda)
, the natural isomorphism between functors
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\mathsf{mod}_{\Lambda}

\gamma_{\Lambda,g}\colon x^{*}[-d](g\cdot-)\xrightarrow{\sim}x^{*}[-d]

is defined to be the one filling the composite diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(\Spec(k))_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(\Spec(k))_{\Lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{mod}_{\Lambda}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\cdot-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{*}[-d]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{*}[-d]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g\cdot-}

(31)

Here, the upper rectangle witnesses the compatibility of the action of
g
with the inverse image functor
x^{*}[-d]
, while the lower triangle comes from the trivialization of the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(\Spec(k))
discussed in Example
3.11
.
In order to check that the natural isomorphisms
\gamma_{\Lambda,g}
are compatible with composition in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
and functorial with respect to
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra homomorphisms (as required in Definition
A.41
), it suffices to check that the same coherence properties hold both for the upper rectangles and for the lower triangles figuring in (
31
).
For the upper rectangles, the two coherence properties follow from the very description of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-group scheme parameterizing the autoequivalences of the six functor formalism
X\mapsto\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.
For the lower triangles, each of the two coherence properties follows from the corresponding coherence property of the trivialization of the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(\Spec(k))
(as stated in Definition
A.26
(1)).
∎

In the following section, we put the categories of motivic local systems just studied into a six functor formalism.
In
[
Jac25
]
, the extra functoriality provided by the six operations leads to a method to establish the exactness of the fundamental sequence in the case of a
k
-rational base-point.
Unfortunately, we were not able to implement this method in the proof of Theorem
3.18
:
the main issue is that, to do so, we would need to know in advance that the forgetful functor (
25
) sends semisimple objects to semisimple objects.
For Ivorra–Morel’s categories, this semi-simplicity condition follows from the existence of a realization into mixed Hodge modules, obtained by Tubach in
[
Tub25
]
(see
[
Jac25
, Lem. 7.6]
).

4
Ayoub’s categories of Nori motivic sheaves

Motivated by the results of Section
3
, it is natural to look for an alternative construction of motivic perverse sheaves based on the motivic Galois action on perverse sheaves of geometric origin.
In fact, it is useful to start from the motivic Galois action on the stable
\infty
-categories of constructible complexes of geometric origin:
there is a natural
\infty
-categorical notion of homotopy-fixed points, which recovers the
1
-categorical notion of equivariant objects when applied to ordinary categories.
This allows us to study the perverse
t
-structure on the stable
\infty
-categories of homotopy-fixed points.
As for the classical categories of perverse sheaves, the associated realization functor turns out to be an equivalence.

As usual, we work over a field
k
of characteristic
0
endowed with a complex embedding
\sigma\colon k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.

4.1
The motivic Galois action on constructible complexes

Let
X
be a
k
-variety.
Recall that Theorem
3.6
describes a canonical action by the spectral motivic Galois group
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}(k)
on the stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
, such that the induced action by the underlying classical group
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
respects the ordinary
t
-structure.
In the same spirit as in Subsection
3.2
, we want to consider the associated category of equivariant objects.

However, the
1
-categorical notion of equivariant object is not adapted to the triangulated setting, due to the usual technical defects of triangulated categories:
in order to get a well-behaved triangulated equivariant category, one has to work in the setting of stable
\infty
-categories.
Let us review Ayoub’s construction of the
\infty
-category of homotopy-fixed points
D_{\textup{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
in a way adapted to our main applications:

Notation 4.1
.

We introduce the category
\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
defined as follows:

•

Objects are classical commutative
\mathbb{Q}
-algebras
\Lambda
.

•

A morphism
\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
is a pair
(\phi,g)
consisting of a
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra homomorphism
\phi\colon\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
and an element
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)(\Lambda^{\prime})
.

•

The composite of two morphisms
(\phi_{1},g_{1})\colon\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
and
(\phi_{2},g_{2})\colon\Lambda^{\prime}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
is defined as

(\phi_{2},g_{2})\circ(\phi_{1},g_{1}):=(\phi_{2}\circ\phi_{1},g_{2}\phi_{1}(g_{1}))\colon\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime\prime}.

There is a natural functor

B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)\colon\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathsf{Grpd}

associating to each
\Lambda\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}
the group of
\Lambda
-valued points
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)(\Lambda)
, regarded as a groupoid.
By construction,
\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
is the
1
-category of coCartesian sections of the associated coCartesian fibration with
1
-categorical fibres

\int_{\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}}B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)\rightarrow\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Here is Ayoub’s construction of homotopy-fixed points under the motivic Galois group:

Construction 4.2

(
[
Ayo21
, Constr. 2.3.4]
)
.

The stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
fits into a functor

\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{?}:\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathsf{Cat}_{\infty},\quad\Lambda\mapsto\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{\Lambda},

with associated coCartesian fibration

\int_{\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}}\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{?}\rightarrow\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}.

The
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
promotes the latter to a coCartesian fibration

\int_{\Lambda\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}}\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{?}\rightarrow\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{A}(k)},

and we define
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
as the associated stable
\infty
-category of coCartesian sections.
∎

More generally, for any full
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable sub-
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}\subset\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
, the same method yields an
\infty
-category of homotopy-fixed points
\mathcal{C}^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
, which is stable as soon as
\mathcal{C}
is stable.

Notation 4.3
.

We let

\omega_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)

denote the forgetful functor defined by evaluating a coCartesian section at the initial object
\mathbb{Q}\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
We use the same notation for the forgetful functor
\mathcal{C}^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\to\mathcal{C}
on a
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable full sub-
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}\subset\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.

The main example of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable sub-
\infty
-category of
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
is given by the constructible complexes:

Lemma 4.4
.

The full sub-
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)\subset\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
is
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable.

Proof.

Indeed, for every
\Lambda\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, the stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{\Lambda}
can be identified with the full sub-
\infty
-category of
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{\Lambda}
spanned by the compact objects.
∎

The functor
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
identifies
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
with the full sub-
\infty
-category of
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
spanned by those objects
K
whose underlying complex
\omega_{X}(K)\in\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
is constructible.

Proposition 4.5

(
[
Ayo21
, Prop. 2.3.5]
)
.

As
X
varies among
k
-varieties, the stable
\infty
-categories
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
inherit the six operations, and the forgetful functors

\omega_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\to\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)

are canonically compatible with them.

In order to appreciate the significance of this result, we need to recall a key property of Voevodsky motives proved by Drew–Gallauer:

Theorem 4.6

(
[
DG22
, Thm. 7.14]
)
.

The six functor formalism
X\mapsto\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X)
is
2
-initial among all six functor formalisms on
k
-varieties taking values in presentable stable
\infty
-categories and satisfying étale descent.

The stable
\infty
-categories
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
are presentable, since so are the
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.
As a consequence:

Corollary 4.7

(
[
Ayo21
, Cor. 2.3.6]
)
.

For every
k
-variety
X
, there exists an enriched Betti realization functor

\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}

such that the Betti realization over
X
factors as

\Bti_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(X)\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}}\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\xrightarrow{\omega_{X}}\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)

up to canonical natural isomorphism.
As
X
varies, the functors
\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}
are canonically compatible with the six operations.

In the following, we are mostly interested in the restriction of the enriched Betti realization to compact motives

\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}.

Note that the sub-
\infty
-categories
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\subset\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
are stable under the six operations, since the analogous property holds for the underlying categories of constructible complexes.
They also inherit Beilinson’s gluing functors from the underlying categories
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.

Informally,
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
can be thought as an
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-representations inside
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
. However, since the representation structure is induced by the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action, not every object of
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
can underlie an object of
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
:

Example 4.8
.

Consider the setting of Example
3.15
in the case when
X=\Spec(k)
and
k^{\prime}/k
is a non-trivial finite Galois extension.
Recall that the complex-analytic space
\Spec(k^{\prime})^{\sigma}
consists of
[k^{\prime}\colon k]
points, indexed by the set
\Hom_{\sigma}(k^{\prime},\mathbb{C})
.
Every constructible complex of geometric origin over it decomposes into skyscraper complexes accordingly.

As a consequence of Corollary
4.7
, the unit sheaf
\mathbb{Q}_{\Spec(k^{\prime})^{\sigma}}=\Bti_{\sigma,\Spec(k^{\prime})}^{*}(\mathbb{Q}_{\Spec(k^{\prime})})\in\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(\Spec(k))
canonically underlies an object of
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(\Spec(k^{\prime}))^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
.
However, its restrictions to the single points of
\Spec(k^{\prime})^{\sigma}
individually do not.
Indeed, the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-representation on
\mathbb{Q}_{\Spec(k^{\prime})^{\sigma}}
factors through
\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
and matches with the natural
\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
-action on
\Spec(k^{\prime})^{\sigma}
.
Since the latter is transitive, we deduce that the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\mathbb{Q}_{\Spec(k^{\prime})^{\sigma}}
permutes the various restrictions transitively.
∎

4.2
The perverse
t
-structure

Recall that the perverse
t
-structure on
\mathit{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
(by Proposition
1.24
), which we indicate here as
(\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\leq_{p}0},\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\geq_{p}0})
.
We want to lift the perverse
t
-structure to Ayoub’s category of homotopy-fixed points and describe its heart explicitly.
Recall from Subsection
1.2
that we have the filtered union

\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)=\bigcup_{\Sigma\in\mathsf{Strat}_{X/k}}\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X,\Sigma)

indexed by the poset
\mathsf{Strat}_{X/k}
of
k
-stratifications.
Following
[
BBD+18
, § 2]
, the perverse
t
-structure on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
can be recovered by first constructing the so-called
\Sigma
-perverse
t
-structure on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X,\Sigma)
for each
\Sigma\in\mathsf{Strat}_{X/k}
and then passing to the colimit, exactly as one does for
\mathit{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)
.

We have the following stability results for the motivic Galois action:

Lemma 4.9
.

For every
\Sigma\in\mathsf{Strat}_{X/k}
, the full sub-
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X,\Sigma)\subset\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
is
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable.

Proof.

Fix
\Lambda\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
The triangulated category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X,\Sigma)_{\Lambda}
can be identified with the full subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{\Lambda}
spanned by those complexes
K
such that, for each stratum
S\in\Sigma
, with inclusion
s\colon S\hookrightarrow X
, the complex
s^{*}K\in\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(S)_{\Lambda}
is dualizable.
Since the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on constructible complexes commutes with inverse images, and the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)(\Lambda)
-action on each
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(S)_{\Lambda}
preserves dualizable objects (being monoidal), the thesis follows.
∎

Corollary 4.10
.

The subcategories
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\leq_{p}0},\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\geq_{p}0}\subset\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
are both
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable.

Proof.

Since the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
respects each
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X,\Sigma)
(by Lemma
4.9
), it suffices to show that the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{A}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X,\Sigma)
respects the
\Sigma
-perverse
t
-structure.
But this follows from the fact that the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action preserves the ordinary
t
-structure on each
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(S)
(by Theorem
3.6
(2)), commutes with inverse images, and respects shifts in the triangulated category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.
∎

This implies formally the existence of the sought-after perverse
t
-structure on homotopy-fixed points:

Proposition 4.11
.

The stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
carries a unique
t
-structure making the forgetful functor
\omega_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)

t
-exact with respect to the perverse
t
-structure on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.

Proof.

Since the forgetful functor
\omega_{X}
is conservative, any
t
-structure as in the statement must be defined by the formula

(\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)})^{\leq_{p}0}:=\omega_{X}^{-1}(\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\leq_{p}0}),\quad(\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)})^{\geq_{p}0}:=\omega_{X}^{-1}(\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\geq_{p}0}).

Hence, the sought-after
t
-structure is uniquely determined, provided it exists.
For every
\Lambda\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, the perverse
t
-structure on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
induced a
t
-structure on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{\Lambda}
(since
\Lambda
is flat over
\mathbb{Q}
).
For every morphism
(\phi,g)\colon\Lambda\to\Lambda^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{A}(k)}
, the induced functor
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
is
t
-exact with respect to these
t
-structures.
We deduce that the limit stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
inherits a uniquely determined compatible
t
-structure.
∎

As a consequence of Corollary
4.10
, the abelian category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\leq_{p}0}\cap\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\geq_{p}0}=:\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
is
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable.
By
[
BBD+18
, Thm. 1.3.6]
, the intersection

(\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)})^{\leq_{p}0}\cap(\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)})^{\geq_{p}0}=\omega_{X}^{-1}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X))

is an admissible abelian subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
, which we refer to as its
perverse heart
.
By construction, it coincides with the essential image of the natural fully faithful functor on homotopy-fixed points

\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}.

In the rest of this paper, we tacitly identify
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
with the perverse heart inside
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
.
The following result offers a more explicit description of this abelian category:

Lemma 4.12
.

The abelian category
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
(defined as in Construction
4.2
) is canonically equivalent to the category of
\mathbb{Q}
-algebraic equivariant objects of Definition
A.22
.

Proof.

The category
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
is defined by considering the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on the
1
-category
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
:
that is to say,
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
is the
1
-category of coCartesian sections of the coCartesian fibration with
1
-categorical fibres

\int_{\Lambda\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)_{\Lambda}\rightarrow\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}.

Note that the associated pseudo-functor
\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\rightarrow\mathsf{Cat_{1}}
is described by the formula

\Lambda\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)_{\Lambda},\quad(\phi,g)\mapsto g\cdot(-\otimes_{\Lambda,\phi}\Lambda^{\prime}).

Unwinding the construction, one sees that the
1
-category
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
admits the following explicit description:

•

Objects are pairs
(\underline{K},\underline{\alpha})
consisting of

–

a family
\underline{K}=(K_{\Lambda})_{\Lambda\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}}
of objects
K_{\Lambda}\in\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)_{\Lambda}
,

–

for every morphism
(\phi,g)\colon\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
, an isomorphism in
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)_{\Lambda^{\prime}}

\alpha_{(\phi,g)}\colon g\cdot(K_{\Lambda}\otimes_{\Lambda,\phi}\Lambda^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}K_{\Lambda^{\prime}}

satisfying the natural cocycle condition with respect to composition of morphisms in
\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
.

•

A morphism
(\underline{K}_{1},\underline{\alpha}_{1})\rightarrow(\mathcal{K}_{2},\underline{\alpha}_{2})
is the datum of

–

for every
\Lambda\in\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
, a morphism
K_{1,\Lambda}\rightarrow K_{2,\Lambda}
in
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)_{\Lambda}

satisfying the natural compatibility condition with respect to morphisms in
\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
.

We claim that the formula

K_{\Lambda}:=K\otimes\Lambda,\quad\alpha_{(\phi,g)}\colon g\cdot((K\otimes\Lambda)\otimes_{\Lambda,\phi}\Lambda^{\prime})=g\cdot(K\otimes\Lambda^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\alpha_{\Lambda^{\prime},g}}K\otimes\Lambda^{\prime}

(32)

defines an equivalence from the category of Definition
A.22
to the latter category of coCartesian sections.
To see this, it suffices to note that every morphism
(\phi,g)\colon\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
in
\mathsf{CAlg}_{B\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
can be factored as

(\phi,g)\colon\Lambda\xrightarrow{(\phi,1_{\Lambda^{\prime}})}\Lambda^{\prime}\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id}_{\Lambda^{\prime}},g)}\Lambda^{\prime}.

Hence, by the cocycle condition, the isomorphism
\alpha_{(\phi,g)}
associated to a coCartesian section
(\underline{K},\underline{\alpha})
can be reconstructed from
\alpha_{(\phi,1_{\Lambda^{\prime}})}\colon K_{\Lambda}\otimes_{\Lambda,\phi}\Lambda^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}K_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
and
\alpha_{(\mathrm{id}_{\Lambda^{\prime}},g)}\colon g\cdot K_{\Lambda^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}K_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
.
Similarly, the compatibility condition for a morphism of coCartesian sections
(\underline{K}_{1},\underline{\alpha}_{1})\rightarrow(\underline{K}_{2},\underline{\alpha}_{2})
with respect to
(\phi,g)
holds as soon as the compatibility conditions with respect to
(\phi,1_{\Lambda^{\prime}})
and
(\mathrm{id}_{\Lambda^{\prime}},g)
hold.
This observation allows one to construct a quasi-inverse to the functor (
32
), thus proving the claim.
∎

Since the inclusion
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)\subset\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
induces a fully faithful inclusion on equivariant objects, we obtain a sequence of fully faithful embeddings

\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\subset\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\subset\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}

compatible with the forgetful functor
\omega_{X}
.
This puts the theory of equivariant local systems of Section
3
into a six functor formalism.
As
X
varies, the abelian categories
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
enjoy the same functoriality as the classical categories of perverse sheaves of geometric origin:
in particular, they are respected by shifted inverse images under smooth morphisms, direct image under closed immersions, extensions by zero and direct images under affine open immersions, the external tensor product, as well as by Beilinson’s gluing functor.

For future reference, let us record the behaviour of the theory under finite extensions of the base field
k
:

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
k^{\prime}/k
be a finite Galois extension.
Given a smooth
k
-variety
X
, set
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{k}k^{\prime}
, and write
e_{X}\colon X^{\prime}\to X
for the corresponding finite Galois covering.
Then:

1.

The abelian categories
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})}
and
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
(defined by regarding
X^{\prime}
as a
k^{\prime}
-variety or as a
k
-variety, respectively) are canonically equivalent.

2.

The inverse image functor
e_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
induces a canonical equivalence

\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)})^{\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)},

where the action of
\textup{Gal}(k^{\prime}/k)
on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\sigma\text{-}\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime})^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
is induced by its action on
X^{\prime}
via
k
-automorphisms.

Proof.

Same proof as for Lemma
3.16
.
∎

4.3
Beilinson’s equivalence

In his celebrated paper
[
Bei87a
]
, Beilinson proved that the bounded derived category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X))
is canonically equivalent to
\mathit{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)
:
the equivalence is given by the realization functor

\real_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X))\rightarrow\mathit{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X),

which extends the natural inclusion
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)\subset\mathit{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)
in degree
0
.
The construction of the realization functor uses the canonical filtered enhancement of the triangulated category
\mathit{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)
, which comes from its structure of stable
\infty
-category.
By construction, Beilinson’s realization restricts to a
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-equivariant triangulated functor

\real_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X))\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X).

The latter is still an equivalence, as one can see by going through Beilinson’s argument:
first one establishes a generic version of the sought-after equivalence, then one deduces the full equivalence arguing by induction.
Indeed, both steps use the six operations as well as the gluing functors, but these preserve sheaves of geometric origin (by Proposition
1.15
).
We refer to
[
Ayo21
, Thm. 1.6.36]
for more details on the proof of the generic equivalence for sheaves of geometric origin.

Beilinson’s result has a natural equivariant version:

Theorem 4.14

(
[
Bei87a
, Thm. 1.3]
)
.

For every
k
-variety
X
, the realization functor

\real_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)})\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}

is an equivalence of stable
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

We ignore whether it is possible to deduce the result formally from the analogous result for classical perverse sheaves (of geometric origin).
However, it suffices to check that Beilinson’s proof goes through in the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-equivariant setting.
This is the case since Beilinson’s argument is based on the six operations and the gluing functors, which lift to the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-equivariant categories;
we leave the details to the interested reader.
∎

We conclude the present section with a complementary result about the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
, which does not play any role in the rest of the article but is nonetheless of independent interest:

Proposition 4.15
.

For every
k
-variety
X
, it is possible to write the stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
as a filtered colimit of the form

\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)=\twocolim_{i\in I}\mathcal{D}_{i}(X)

where
(\mathcal{D}_{i}(X))_{i\in I}
is a filtered direct system of stable
\infty
-categories endowed with compatible
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-actions such that the action of the pro-algebraic group
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
on each
\mathcal{D}_{i}(X)
factors through some algebraic quotient
Q_{i}
.

Proof.

Since Beilinson’s equivalence

\real_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X))\xrightarrow{\sim}\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)

is
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-equivariant, it suffices to prove the thesis for the derived
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X))
.
Moreover, since the formation of bounded derived categories is compatible with filtered colimits of abelian categories (see
[
Gal21
, Lem. 2.6]
), it suffices to prove the analogous statement for the abelian category
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
:
we claim that the latter can be written as a filtered union of the form

\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)=\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathcal{A}_{i}(X)

where each
\mathcal{A}_{i}
is a
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable full abelian subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
on which the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action factors through some algebraic quotient
Q_{i}
.
For this, we proceed as follows.

First, write
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
as the filtered union

\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)=\bigcup_{\Sigma\in\mathsf{Strat}_{X/k}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X,\Sigma).

Since each
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X,\Sigma)=\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X,\Sigma)\cap\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
is
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable (by Lemma
4.4
and Corollary
4.10
), it suffices to prove the claim for each
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X,\Sigma)
individually in place of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
.

So fix one stratification
\Sigma\in\mathsf{Strat}_{X/k}
.
Recall that, by definition, all strata
S\in\Sigma
are smooth over
k
and connected.
For each stratum
S\in\Sigma
, there is a finite field extension
k^{\prime}/k
such that the connected components of the smooth
k^{\prime}
-variety
S\times_{k}k^{\prime}
are geometrically connected over
k^{\prime}

(take
k^{\prime}
to be the algebraic closure of
k
inside the function field
k(S)
).
Clearly, for every further finite extension
k^{\prime\prime}/k^{\prime}
, the
k^{\prime\prime}
-variety
S\times_{k}k^{\prime\prime}
enjoys the same property with respect to
k^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence, it is possible to choose a finite extension
k^{\prime}/k
which works for all strata
S\in\Sigma
at once.
Now consider the
k^{\prime}
-variety
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{k}k^{\prime}
.
Note that the collection of all connected components of
S\times_{k}k^{\prime}
for each
S\in\Sigma
defines a
k^{\prime}
-algebraic stratification
\Sigma^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Strat}_{X^{\prime}/k^{\prime}}
in the sense of Notation
1.10
:
indeed, property (i) holds by construction, property (ii) follows from the corresponding property for
\Sigma
, and property (iii) follows from the inclusions between Zariski closures

\overline{S\times_{k}k^{\prime}}\subset\bar{S}\times_{k}k^{\prime}

inside
X^{\prime}
.
Now choose a complex embedding
\sigma^{\prime}\colon k^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
extending
\sigma\colon k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.
The
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})
-equivariant equivalence of Lemma
1.27
restricts to a fully faithful inclusion

\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X,\Sigma)\subset\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime}).

Since
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})
has finite index in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
(by Proposition
3.5
), it suffices to prove the claim for the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k^{\prime})
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime})
.
In other words, up to replacing the
k
-variety
X
by the
k^{\prime}
-variety
X^{\prime}
and
\Sigma
by
\Sigma^{\prime}
, we may assume that all strata in
\Sigma
are geometrically connected over
k
.

In this situation, for each stratum
S\in\Sigma
there is a finite extension
k^{\prime}/k
such that the geometrically connected
k^{\prime}
-variety
S\times_{k}k^{\prime}
admits a
k^{\prime}
-rational point.
As before, it is possible to choose a finite extension
k^{\prime}/k
which works for all strata
S\in\Sigma
at once.
Up to replacing the
k
-variety
X
by the
k^{\prime}
-variety
X^{\prime}:=X\times_{k}k^{\prime}
and the stratification
\Sigma
by the corresponding stratification
\Sigma^{\prime}
as in the previous step, we may assume that each stratum in
\Sigma
admits a
k
-rational point.

Since the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on sheaves of geometric origin commutes with the six operations, the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X,\Sigma)
is compatible with restriction to each stratum
S\in\Sigma
.
Hence, it suffices to prove the claim for the abelian category
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(S)
for each
S
individually in place of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X,\Sigma)
.
In other words, up to replacing
X
by
S
, we may assume that
X
is smooth and geometrically connected over
k
, with a rational point
x\in X(k)
, and that
\Sigma=\left\{X\right\}
.

Finally, in this situation, we prove the claim for the abelian category
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)=\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X,\left\{X\right\})
.
Following Notation
2.18
, let
\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)
denote the Tannaka dual of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
at the
k
-rational point
x
.
As explained in the proof of Theorem
3.18
, the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
is induced by an algebraic action of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
on
\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)
(in the sense of Example
A.33
).
Form the resulting semi-direct product

\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
,
and let
\left\{G_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
denote
the filtered poset of all its algebraic quotients;
for each
i\in I
, let

K_{i}
and
Q_{i}

denote the image of

\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)
and
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
,
respectively,
in
G_{i}
.
Since the algebraic group
K_{i}
is a quotient of
\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)
, it is the Tannaka dual of a
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-stable Tannakian subcategory
\mathcal{A}_{i}
of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
.
By construction, the
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
-action on
\mathcal{A}_{i}
is induced by the conjugation action of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)
on
K_{i}
, and the latter factors through its algebraic quotient
Q_{i}
.
Lastly, since
K_{i}
is algebraic, the Tannakian category
\mathcal{A}_{i}
is tensor-generated by one object
L_{i}\in\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
.
But, since
\pi_{1}^{\rm{A}}(X,x)
is a closed subgroup of the semi-direct product, every object of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
is a subquotient of some representation of the semi-direct product (by
[
DM82
, Prop. 2.21(b)]
).
This implies that we have the filtered union

\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)=\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathcal{A}_{i},

as wanted.
∎

5
The comparison theorem

The present section contains the main result of this paper, Theorem
5.4
below:
we show that Ayoub’s categories of equivariant sheaves under the motivic Galois group recover Ivorra–Morel’s categories in a very precise sense.

Exploiting the universal property of Ivorra–Morel’s categories, we easily construct a system of comparison functors to Ayoub’s categories:
our main result asserts that these functors are all equivalences.
Using the six functor formalism, we deduce the result from its generic variant, asserting that our comparison functors induce equivalences at the generic points of all
k
-varieties.
As a byproduct, we show that the two notions of perverse sheaf of geometric origin introduced in the previous sections define the same category, and similarly for the two notions of local system of geometric origin.

As usual, we work over a field
k
of characteristic
0
endowed with a complex embedding
\sigma\colon k\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
.

5.1
Construction of the comparison functors

Let
X
be a (quasi-projective)
k
-variety.
Recall from Section
2.1
that Ivorra–Morel’s category
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)
is universal among all abelian categories factoring the homological functor
{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\Bti_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)
.
Since the image of the latter is contained in
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
, the forgetful functor
\iota_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)
factors through
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)
as well;
for sake of simplicity, we use the same notation for the refined forgetful functor

\iota_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X).

As we explain now, the latter factors through the category of equivariant objects
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
.
The idea is that Ayoub’s construction defines a theory of enhanced perverse sheaves through which the Betti realization factors.

Notation 5.1
.

For sake of simplicity, in the rest of the present section we write the category of equivariant objects
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
as
\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)
.
Similarly, when
X
is smooth over
k
, we write its full subcategory
\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}_{\mathrm{geo}}^{\rm{A}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
as
\operatorname{\mathcal{P}\operatorname{\mathsf{Loc}}}(X)
.

Construction 5.2
.

Recall that Ayoub’s Corollary
4.7
provides a factorization of the Betti realization
\Bti_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)\to\mathit{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)
as

\Bti_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}}\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\xrightarrow{\omega_{X}}\mathit{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X).

We deduce that the homological functor
{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\Bti_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)
factors as

{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\Bti_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)\xrightarrow{{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}}\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)\xrightarrow{\omega_{X}}\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)

up to natural isomorphism.
The abelian category
\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)
, together with the homological functor
{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)
and the faithful exact functor
\omega_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)
, satisfies the assumptions on the abelian category
\mathcal{A}(X)
appearing in the statement of the universal property of
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)
in Subsection
2.1
;
the natural isomorphism between functors
\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)

\kappa_{X}\colon{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\Bti_{X}^{*}\xrightarrow{\sim}\omega_{X}\circ({}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*})={}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ(\omega_{X}\circ\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*})

mentioned in the statement is induced by the natural isomorphism
\Bti_{X}^{*}\xrightarrow{\sim}\omega_{X}\circ\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}
.
Applying the universal property of
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)
, we obtain a canonical faithful exact functor

o_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)

making the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{X}}

commute up to the induced natural isomorphism
\tilde{\kappa}_{X}\colon\iota_{X}\xrightarrow{\sim}\omega_{X}\circ o_{X}
.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

As mentioned in Section
2.1
, in this situation
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)
can be identified with the universal abelian factorization of the homological functor
{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}
.

Passing to the bounded derived categories, the functor
o_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)
extends to a conservative triangulated functor

o_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X))\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X))

making the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{X}}

commute up to canonical natural isomorphism.
We can finally state our comparison result as follows:

Theorem 5.4
.

For every (quasi-projective)
k
-variety
X
, the triangulated comparison functor

o_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X))\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X))=\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}

is an equivalence.

The equivalence
\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X))=\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}
appearing in the formula is the one of Theorem
4.14
.

The rest of the present section is devoted to proving the comparison theorem.
For sake of simplicity, in the following we limit ourselves to considering quasi-projective
k
-varieties;
since the assignments
X\mapsto\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)
and
X\mapsto\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)
, regarded as fibered categories over open immersions between
k
-varieties, are both stacks for the Zariski topology, the equivalence over general bases follows formally from the equivalence over quasi-projective (or even just affine) bases.
The advantage of working in the quasi-projective setting is that we can exploit the six functor formalism of Ivorra–Morel’s categories systematically.

The following observation plays a crucial role in each step of the proof of the comparison theorem:

Proposition 5.5
.

As
X
varies among (quasi-projective)
k
-varieties, the triangulated comparison functors
o_{X}
are canonically compatible with the six operations, as well as with Beilinson’s gluing functors.

Essentially, this is just a formal consequence of how the six operations and the gluing functors on Ivorra–Morel’s derived categories are constructed:
indeed, one can repeat the whole construction of
[
IM24
]
and
[
Ter24b
]
using the description of
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)
as the universal abelian factorization of
{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\widetilde{\Bti}_{X}^{*}
in place of its original definition in terms of
{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}\circ\Bti_{X}^{*}
(see Remark
5.3
above).
To make this point clear, let us discuss the case of inverse images under open immersions in detail, along the lines of Example
2.4
:

Example 5.6
.

Fix an open immersion of (quasi-projective)
k
-varieties
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
.
The triangulated functor

j^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(U)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}

is
t
-exact for the perverse
t
-structure defined in Section
4.2
(as the underlying functor on constructible complexes is
t
-exact).
Thus, it restricts to an exact functor

j^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(U),

and in fact, by
[
Vol10
, Thm. 1]
, it can be identified with the derived functor

j^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X))\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(U))

modulo the equivalences of Theorem
4.14
.
Since the upper half of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(U)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(U)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Bti}^{*}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Bti}^{*}_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\mathrm{p}}\!\operatorname{\mathit{H}}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}

(33)

commutes up to canonical natural isomorphism (by Corollary
4.7
), the same holds for the outer rectangle.
Applying
[
Ter24a
, Prop. 2.5]
, one gets a canonical exact functor

j^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(U)

(34)

rendering both halves of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(U)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}

(35)

commutative up to natural isomorphism.
More precisely, (
34
) is the unique exact functor which makes the upper half of (
35
) commute on the nose (see Remark
2.5
), hence it coincides with the functor (
12
) of Example
2.4
.
The natural isomorphism filling the lower half of (
35
) is then uniquely determined by its compatibility with the natural isomorphism filling (
33
) (see again Remark
2.5
):
this means that the composite of the natural isomorphisms filling the two halves of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(U)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}

coincides with the natural isomorphism filling the lower half of (
13
).
Passing to the bounded derived categories, we obtain a similar decomposition of (
14
) as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(U))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(U))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(U)).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{*}}

The natural isomorphism filling the upper half of the latter diagram witnesses the compatibility of the comparison functors with the inverse image functors
j^{*}
.
By construction, these natural isomorphisms are compatible with composition of open immersions, so they define a morphism of triangulated fibered categories over the category of open immersions between
k
-varieties.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.5
.

The argument in Example
5.6
only uses the
t
-exactness of inverse images under open immersions.
The same argument applies to shifted inverse images under smooth morphisms and to direct images under closed immersions:
this yields analogous natural isomorphisms witnessing the compatibility of the comparison functors with these two classes of functors, compatibly with composition in each of the two classes separately.
Starting from these two partial definitions, and repeating the arguments of
[
IM24
, § 4.2]
word-by-word, one obtains a canonical system of natural isomorphisms witnessing the compatibility of the comparison functors with inverse images under arbitrary morphisms of (quasi-projective)
k
-varieties.

To this end, in view of how inverse images under closed immersions are constructed in
[
IM24
, § 4.1]
, one needs to know in advance that the comparison functors are compatible with Beilinson’s gluing functors, in a similar way.
But, since the gluing functors on perverse Nori motives are defined by direct application of the lifting result
[
Ter24a
, Prop. 2.5]
, the needed compatibility is obtained exactly as in the case of open immersions.
Once the compatibility of the comparison functors with inverse images is established, their compatibility with the four operations of type
f^{*}
,
f_{*}
,
f_{!}
and
f^{!}
follows formally:
for instance, compatibility with direct images follows from the validity of the same property after composition with the conservative functors
\omega_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\to\mathit{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(X)
.

The above argument for inverse images under open immersion and, more generally, for
t
-exact functors, extends naturally to the case of multilinear functors which are
t
-exact in each variable (using
[
Ter24a
, § 4]
).
In particular, for every choice of (quasi-projective)
k
-varieties
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
, one obtains a new construction of the external tensor product functor

-\boxtimes-\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X_{1})\times\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X_{2})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X_{1}\times X_{2}),

in such a way that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X_{1})\times\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(X_{1}\times X_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X_{1})\times\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(X_{1}\times X_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\boxtimes-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{X_{1}}\times o_{X_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces o_{X_{1}\times X_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\boxtimes-}

commutes up to natural isomorphism.
In order to see that the natural isomorphisms just constructed are compatible with inverse image functors as well as with associativity, commutativity, and unit constraints, it now suffices to repeat the arguments of
[
Ter24b
, §§ 2,4]
word-by-word.
This shows that the triangulated comparison functors are unitary symmetric monoidal, compatibly with inverse images.
Moreover, they automatically respect internal homomorphisms, because, again, this is true after composition with the conservative functors
\omega_{X}\colon\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)^{\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)}\to\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{geo}}(X)
.
∎

5.2
Comparison over a point

The first step in the proof of Theorem
5.4
is to establish the sought-after equivalence in the case when
X=\Spec(k)
or, more generally, when
X=\Spec(k^{\prime})
for a finite extension
k^{\prime}/k
.

Recall from Example
3.11
that the Tannakian category
\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(k)
is canonically equivalent to
\Rep_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k))
.
Since the comparison functor

o_{k}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(k)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{P}}(k)

is canonically monoidal (by Proposition
5.5
) and compatible with the forgetful functors to
\operatorname{\mathsf{Perv}}(\Spec(k))=\mathsf{vect}_{\mathbb{Q}}
(by construction), it determines a homomorphism of Tannaka dual groups

\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{A}}(k)\to\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{N}}(k),

and saying that
o_{k}
is an equivalence amounts to saying that this homomorphism is an isomorphism.
Before proceeding further, we need a digression on the relation between Nori’s and Ayoub’s motivic Galois groups.
For sake of simplicity, we limit ourselves to the motivic Galois group of the base field
k
, but of course the same discussion applies to finite extensions of
k
as well.

The starting point is the following important result:

Theorem 5.7

(
[
HM17
, Thm. 10.1.1]
,
[
CG17
, Prop. 7.12]
)
.

There exists a canonical tensor-triangulated functor

\Nri_{k}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(k)\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(k))

such that the Betti realization over
k
factors as

\Bti_{k}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}_{\mathrm{ct}}(k)\xrightarrow{\Nri_{k}^{*}}\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(k))\xrightarrow{\iota_{k}}\operatorname{\mathit{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathsf{vect}_{\mathbb{Q}})

up to monoidal natural isomorphism.

This was essentially proved by Nori, using his own construction of
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(k)
.
The fact that Nori’s category coincides with the universal abelian factorization of the homological functor
H^{0}\circ\Bti_{k}^{*}
(as in Ivorra–Morel’s definition) is a consequence of this result.

Let
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{N}}(k))
denote the Hopf algebra of regular functions on the pro-algebraic group
\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{N}}(k)
.
By construction, Nori’s realization functor extends canonically to a tensor-triangulated functor

\Nri_{k}^{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(k)\rightarrow D(\Ind\operatorname{\mathcal{M}}(k))=\mathsf{coMod}_{\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{N}}(k))}(D(\mathbb{Q}))

making the two halves of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{coMod}_{\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{N}}(k))}(D(\mathbb{Q}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathbb{Q})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Nri_{k}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bti_{k}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textup{for}_{\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{N}}(k))}}

commute up to monoidal natural isomorphism.
Recall from Section
3.1
that Ayoub’s derived Hopf algebra
\mathcal{H}_{k}
is universal among all Hopf algebras giving rise to a commutative diagram of this form.
Therefore, we obtain a canonical bialgebra morphism in
D(\mathbb{Q})

\mathcal{H}_{k}\to\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{N}}(k))

(36)

making the two halves of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathbf{DA}}^{\textup{\'{e}t}}(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{coMod}_{\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{mot}}^{\mathrm{N}}(k))}(D(\mathbb{Q}))}
